Maybe… yes, in a loose way. I don’t mean I have it all figured out or anything like that. It’s just that the watching and the little messages sort of feel like they’re about the same thing—about me. I know that probably doesn’t make much sense.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually watching you and sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to say I know it for sure. It feels like some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. Not every minute or anything, and I’ve tried not to make a fuss about it, but the feeling of being watched—and those little messages—have been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages you think you're seeing are actually about you, or could they be coincidences?\n\nPatient: I… I know they could be coincidences. I do try to tell myself that. But honestly, even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of it really is about me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that probably sounds silly… but it feels hard to dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Are these beliefs about being watched and seeing messages organized into a coherent story or explanation in your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really a whole story, no… I don’t have some big explanation worked out. It’s more just this feeling I can’t get rid of, that some people at church or in my walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things people say seem like messages about me. I know that probably doesn’t make much sense.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually watching you and sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to say I know it for sure. But even when I’m having a pretty good day, I can’t shake it. It feels like some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. I know that may sound odd… I’m just trying to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're seeing become more organized or connected in your mind over the eleven months you've been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s become, um… more organized exactly. Not like I have a whole clear explanation for it. It’s just stayed with me, even on better days—that feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that probably sounds a bit much… but that’s the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like all these things - being watched and the messages - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, in a loose way. I don’t mean I have it all figured out or anything like that. It’s just that the watching and the little messages sort of feel like they’re about the same thing—about me. Like at church, or with the walking club, something in the bulletin or something someone says will feel connected to that feeling that they’re watching me.\n\nI know that may not sound very reasonable. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it’s more than just once in a while, I think.\n\nEven on my better days, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. So… maybe not constantly, but it’s there a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. But it does come up more than I wish it did.

I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.

I still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. But I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings, and I haven’t been as involved with the walking club. Even my gardening has slipped, and I used to really enjoy that.

I do try to tell myself that. I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.

I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it’s more than just once in a while, I think.\n\nEven on my better days, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. So… maybe not constantly, but it’s there a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that people are watching you or sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… not all the time. I do try to question it, because I know it probably sounds strange.\n\nBut even on my better days, there are times when it feels very real to me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. In those moments it’s hard to let it go.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m absolutely certain all the time, but when it comes on, it really does bother me and feels real. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. But it does come up more than I wish it did.\n\nEspecially if I’ve been to church, or with the walking club, or if I’m looking at the bulletin… then I can spend quite a while turning it over in my mind, wondering if people are watching me or if something said was meant as a little message about me.\n\nI don’t know if I’m estimating right, but maybe it adds up to an hour or two some days? Less if I’m distracted. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but it’s hard for me to completely shake it. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has believing you're being watched or seeing these messages affected your ability to participate in activities you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.\n\nI still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. But I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings, and I haven’t been as involved with the walking club. Even my gardening has slipped, and I used to really enjoy that.\n\nMy daughter has also started checking in more, making sure I’m eating properly and keeping track of appointments. She’s kind about it, but I suppose that means it has affected me some.\n\nIs that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you ever think that it's definitely, absolutely true, without any doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.\n\nBut when it’s happening, especially at church or with the walking club, or when I see something in the bulletin, it can feel very much like it’s really about me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m probably making too much of it, but I came because I’ve been feeling like some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. She’s kind about it, but I suppose that means it has affected me some.\n\nIs that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you ever think that it's definitely, absolutely true, without any doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.\n\nBut when it’s happening, especially at church or with the walking club, or when I see something in the bulletin, it can feel very much like it’s really about me. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m probably making too much of it, but I came because I’ve been feeling like some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake it. Sometimes it feels like there are little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things people say. I know that may sound strange… I just thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like people are watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I guess about eleven months now. Not every minute or anything, and I’ve tried not to make a fuss about it, but the feeling of being watched—and those little messages—have been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages you think you're seeing are actually about you, or could they be coincidences?\n\nPatient: I… I know they could be coincidences. I do try to tell myself that. But honestly, even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of it really is about me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that probably sounds silly… but it feels hard to dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Are these beliefs about being watched and seeing messages organized into a coherent story or explanation in your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really a whole story, no… I don’t have some big explanation worked out. It’s more just this feeling I can’t get rid of, that some people at church or in my walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things people say seem like messages about me. I know that probably doesn’t make much sense.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually watching you and sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to say I know it for sure. But even when I’m having a pretty good day, I can’t shake it. It feels like some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. I know that may sound odd… I’m just trying to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're seeing become more organized or connected in your mind over the eleven months you've been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s become, um… more organized exactly. Not like I have a whole clear explanation for it. It’s just stayed with me, even on better days—that feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that probably sounds a bit much… but that’s the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like all these things - being watched and the messages - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, in a loose way. I don’t mean I have it all figured out or anything like that. It’s just that the watching and the little messages sort of feel like they’re about the same thing—about me. Like at church, or with the walking club, something in the bulletin or something someone says will feel connected to that feeling that they’re watching me.\n\nI know that may not sound very reasonable. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it’s more than just once in a while, I think.\n\nEven on my better days, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. So… maybe not constantly, but it’s there a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that people are watching you or sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… not all the time. I do try to question it, because I know it probably sounds strange.\n\nBut even on my better days, there are times when it feels very real to me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. In those moments it’s hard to let it go.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m absolutely certain all the time, but when it comes on, it really does bother me and feels real. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. But it does come up more than I wish it did.\n\nEspecially if I’ve been to church, or with the walking club, or if I’m looking at the bulletin… then I can spend quite a while turning it over in my mind, wondering if people are watching me or if something said was meant as a little message about me.\n\nI don’t know if I’m estimating right, but maybe it adds up to an hour or two some days? Less if I’m distracted. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but it’s hard for me to completely shake it. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has believing you're being watched or seeing these messages affected your ability to participate in activities you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.\n\nI still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. But I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings, and I haven’t been as involved with the walking club. Even my gardening has slipped, and I used to really enjoy that.\n\nMy daughter has also started checking in more, making sure I’m eating properly and keeping track of appointments. She’s kind about it, but I suppose that means it has affected me some.\n\nIs that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you ever think that it's definitely, absolutely true, without any doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.\n\nBut when it’s happening, especially at church or with the walking club, or when I see something in the bulletin, it can feel very much like it’s really about me. Like people are watching me or slipping in little messages. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief in being watched or receiving messages is something that might be considered irrational or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose other people might think it sounds unrealistic. I can understand that, when I’m saying it out loud.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things people say.\n\nI know that may not sound reasonable to someone else. I’m not trying to be difficult… it just feels hard to let go of. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you think this is something that might be possible but not certain, or do you think it's something that is definitely happening?\n\nPatient: I think… I’d say it feels possible, but not completely certain. I don’t want to say I know it’s definitely happening, because I do try to question it.\n\nBut even on better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It feels real enough that it stays with me, if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you, do you think it's something that might be possible or something that is definitely happening?\n\nPatient: I’d say… more that it feels possible, not something I can prove is definitely happening.\n\nBut when it comes up, especially with people at church or in the walking club, I have a hard time shaking it. Like maybe they’re watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that probably sounds like I’m reading too much into things… but it does feel real to me in the moment.\n\nIs that answering it the right way?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m probably making too much of it, but I came because I’ve been feeling like some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake it. Sometimes it feels like there are little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things people say. I know that may sound strange… I just thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like people are watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I guess about eleven months now. Not every minute or anything, and I’ve tried not to make a fuss about it, but the feeling of being watched—and those little messages—have been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages you think you're seeing are actually about you, or could they be coincidences?\n\nPatient: I… I know they could be coincidences. I do try to tell myself that. But honestly, even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of it really is about me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that probably sounds silly… but it feels hard to dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Are these beliefs about being watched and seeing messages organized into a coherent story or explanation in your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really a whole story, no… I don’t have some big explanation worked out. It’s more just this feeling I can’t get rid of, that some people at church or in my walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things people say seem like messages about me. I know that probably doesn’t make much sense.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually watching you and sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to say I know it for sure. But even when I’m having a pretty good day, I can’t shake it. It feels like some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. I know that may sound odd… I’m just trying to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're seeing become more organized or connected in your mind over the eleven months you've been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s become, um… more organized exactly. Not like I have a whole clear explanation for it. It’s just stayed with me, even on better days—that feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that probably sounds a bit much… but that’s the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like all these things - being watched and the messages - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, in a loose way. I don’t mean I have it all figured out or anything like that. It’s just that the watching and the little messages sort of feel like they’re about the same thing—about me. Like at church, or with the walking club, something in the bulletin or something someone says will feel connected to that feeling that they’re watching me.\n\nI know that may not sound very reasonable. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it’s more than just once in a while, I think.\n\nEven on my better days, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I do try to question it, because I know it probably sounds strange.\n\nBut even on my better days, there are times when it feels very real to me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. In those moments it’s hard to let it go.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m absolutely certain all the time, but when it comes on, it really does bother me and feels real. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. Less if I’m distracted. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but it’s hard for me to completely shake it. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has believing you're being watched or seeing these messages affected your ability to participate in activities you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.\n\nI still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. But I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings, and I haven’t been as involved with the walking club. Even my gardening has slipped, and I used to really enjoy that.\n\nMy daughter has also started checking in more, making sure I’m eating properly and keeping track of appointments. She’s kind about it, but I suppose that means it has affected me some.\n\nIs that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you ever think that it's definitely, absolutely true, without any doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.\n\nBut when it’s happening, especially at church or with the walking club, or when I see something in the bulletin, it can feel very much like it’s really about me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m probably making too much of it, but I came because I’ve been feeling like some people at church and in my walking club are watching me. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I can’t quite shake it. Sometimes it feels like there are little messages about me in the church bulletin, or in things people say. I know that may sound strange… I just thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like people are watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I guess about eleven months now. Not every minute or anything, and I’ve tried not to make a fuss about it, but the feeling of being watched—and those little messages—have been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these messages you think you're seeing are actually about you, or could they be coincidences?\n\nPatient: I… I know they could be coincidences. I do try to tell myself that. But honestly, even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some of it really is about me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that probably sounds silly… but it feels hard to dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Are these beliefs about being watched and seeing messages organized into a coherent story or explanation in your mind?\n\nPatient: Not really a whole story, no… I don’t have some big explanation worked out. It’s more just this feeling I can’t get rid of, that some people at church or in my walking club are watching me, and sometimes the bulletin or little things people say seem like messages about me. I know that probably doesn’t make much sense.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that people are actually watching you and sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely certain… I don’t want to say I know it for sure. But even when I’m having a pretty good day, I can’t shake it. It feels like some people at church and in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things they say. I know that may sound odd… I’m just trying to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being watched and the messages you think you're seeing become more organized or connected in your mind over the eleven months you've been experiencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s become, um… more organized exactly. Not like I have a whole clear explanation for it. It’s just stayed with me, even on better days—that feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, and that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that probably sounds a bit much… but that’s the part I can’t seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like all these things - being watched and the messages - are connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, in a loose way. I don’t mean I have it all figured out or anything like that. It’s just that the watching and the little messages sort of feel like they’re about the same thing—about me. Like at church, or with the walking club, something in the bulletin or something someone says will feel connected to that feeling that they’re watching me.\n\nI know that may not sound very reasonable. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is… it’s just hard for me to shake, even when I’m otherwise doing okay.\n\nDoctor: When you think about these messages and people watching you, do you ever doubt that they're really happening?\n\nPatient: I do doubt it sometimes, yes… I mean, I know it might just be me reading too much into things. I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people.\n\nBut even when I tell myself that, I still can’t quite shake the feeling—like some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. It’s not like I’m trying to make trouble… it just keeps coming back in my mind.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think these people are actually watching you or sending you messages, most of the time or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say every minute or anything… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But it’s more than just once in a while, I think.\n\nEven on my better days, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. So… maybe not constantly, but it’s there a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that people are watching you or sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… not all the time. I do try to question it, because I know it probably sounds strange.\n\nBut even on my better days, there are times when it feels very real to me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. In those moments it’s hard to let it go.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m absolutely certain all the time, but when it comes on, it really does bother me and feels real. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. But it does come up more than I wish it did.\n\nEspecially if I’ve been to church, or with the walking club, or if I’m looking at the bulletin… then I can spend quite a while turning it over in my mind, wondering if people are watching me or if something said was meant as a little message about me.\n\nI don’t know if I’m estimating right, but maybe it adds up to an hour or two some days? Less if I’m distracted. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but it’s hard for me to completely shake it. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has believing you're being watched or seeing these messages affected your ability to participate in activities you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.\n\nI still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. But I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings, and I haven’t been as involved with the walking club. Even my gardening has slipped, and I used to really enjoy that.\n\nMy daughter has also started checking in more, making sure I’m eating properly and keeping track of appointments. She’s kind about it, but I suppose that means it has affected me some.\n\nIs that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you ever think that it's definitely, absolutely true, without any doubt at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “absolutely” all the time… I mean, I try to leave room that maybe I’m mistaken.\n\nBut when it’s happening, especially at church or with the walking club, or when I see something in the bulletin, it can feel very much like it’s really about me. Like people are watching me or slipping in little messages. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief in being watched or receiving messages is something that might be considered irrational or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose other people might think it sounds unrealistic. I can understand that, when I’m saying it out loud.\n\nIt’s just that I came in because even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things people say.\n\nI know that may not sound reasonable to someone else. I’m not trying to be difficult… it just feels hard to let go of. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are watching you or sending you messages, do you think this is something that might be possible but not certain, or do you think it's something that is definitely happening?\n\nPatient: I think… I’d say it feels possible, but not completely certain. So… maybe not constantly, but it’s there a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: How often do you feel completely convinced that people are watching you or sending you these messages?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… not all the time. I do try to question it, because I know it probably sounds strange.\n\nBut even on my better days, there are times when it feels very real to me—like people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I guess I’m not completely sure every moment, but the feeling comes up a lot and it’s hard to shake.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you do feel convinced that people are watching you or sending you messages, how strong is that feeling?\n\nPatient: It can feel pretty strong when it happens… stronger than I’d like to admit, I guess.\n\nI try to tell myself maybe I’m just taking things the wrong way, but then I’ll be at church or with the walking club, or I’ll read the bulletin, and it feels like there are little messages about me or that people are watching me. In those moments it’s hard to let it go.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m absolutely certain all the time, but when it comes on, it really does bother me and feels real. Is that clear enough?\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend thinking about these messages and the feeling of being watched on a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um… on a typical day, I’d say it’s not the whole day. I can still do other things. But it does come up more than I wish it did.\n\nEspecially if I’ve been to church, or with the walking club, or if I’m looking at the bulletin… then I can spend quite a while turning it over in my mind, wondering if people are watching me or if something said was meant as a little message about me.\n\nI don’t know if I’m estimating right, but maybe it adds up to an hour or two some days? Less if I’m distracted. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but it’s hard for me to completely shake it. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has believing you're being watched or seeing these messages affected your ability to participate in activities you enjoy?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. Maybe more than I like to admit.\n\nI still live at home and I manage some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t function. Like people are watching me or slipping in little messages. Even on better days, I can’t quite shake that feeling.\n\nI hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that your belief in being watched or receiving messages is something that might be considered irrational or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose other people might think it sounds unrealistic. Like maybe they’re watching me, or there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says.
